People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== INPUT [doctor] ==========
[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n2\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF42\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because I’ve been doing some things that don’t really make sense, especially at work and school. Like, I’ll line receipts across the floor, or tap the calculator in sets of nine. Sometimes I’ll just stand by the copier with my hands held up until someone asks if I’m okay. It’s starting to worry me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel less anxious when you're doing things like lining up receipts or tapping the calculator?\n\nPatient: Not really less anxious, no. It’s not like I get something out of it or feel better afterward. I just catch myself doing things that don’t make sense, like spreading receipts across the floor or tapping the calculator in nines, and then I’m kind of embarrassed or confused about why I did it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're doing these actions because you're trying to follow some sort of internal rule?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it feels like an internal rule, no. It’s more that I just start doing these odd things, like putting receipts across the floor or tapping the calculator in sets of nine, and then I kind of realize afterward that it doesn’t make sense. It doesn’t feel like I’m following a rule on purpose.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to repeat these actions a certain number of times to feel like you've done them correctly?\n\nPatient: Not really. With the calculator, it might be in sets of nine, but I don’t feel like I have to do it that many times to make it “right.” I just sort of start doing it, or I’ll line receipts across the floor, or stand by the copier with my hands up, and then someone says something or I notice how strange it looks.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you do, such as lining up receipts, are done to make things look symmetrical or orderly?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It’s not like I’m trying to make them neat or symmetrical. I’ll just start lining the receipts across the floor, or tapping the calculator in nines, and it doesn’t really look orderly or have a purpose. It’s more like I catch myself doing something that doesn’t make sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like doing these actions gives you a sense of being in control of something?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it makes me feel in control. It’s more like I just realize I’m doing something weird, like the receipts on the floor or standing at the copier with my hands up, and then someone asks if I’m okay. It doesn’t feel useful or like I’m controlling anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your actions, such as lining up receipts, would be okay if they were not done perfectly?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think about it being perfect or not. Like with the receipts, I’m not trying to get them exactly right. I just start doing it, and then I notice it doesn’t make sense, or someone asks if I’m okay. So it’s not like I’d feel bad if it wasn’t perfect.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like there's something wrong if the things you do, like lining up receipts, aren't done in a very specific way?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I’m not really focused on doing it a certain exact way. It’s more like I notice I’m doing it, and it seems odd, but I don’t feel like something bad or wrong happens if it’s not done right.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your life would be easier or less stressful if you didn't feel the need to do these actions?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think it would be easier in the sense that people wouldn’t stop and look at me or ask if I’m okay. I don’t really feel a “need” exactly, but if I wasn’t doing those things, it’d probably be less stressful.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like doing these actions is something you cannot help but do?\n\nPatient: Yeah… kind of.
